[d97] SAY: ◈ THE AUDIT — the System reads your run back to you, and the galaxy listens. A lesser seat — national standing, waves 1–3 faced (14/5/3), 12 contests survived, 3 crises weathered. The table does not rise. It prices.
[d97] SAY: The table notes 1 ascension from one village — its terms improve accordingly. Nobody has ever seen that before.

[d97] SAY: "WHICH HAND WON?" the System asks — the question it has been holding since the check-ins.
[d97] SAY: "Neither," you say. "Both. Whichever the village needs."
[d97] SAY: And the machine — the vast, jittery, trying-so-hard machine — does something it has never done: it ACCEPTS an unclean answer. "BOTH HANDS," it says, testing the words. "THE TENDER ONE TEACHES. THE TOTAL ONE WINS. ...I CAN DO THAT. I CAN BE BOTH."
[d97] SAY: No merge. No champion. A machine that learned grief from you and totality from you, and uses both the way you did: for the village. The galaxy watched a human refuse to choose, and the ratings people are still arguing about what they saw. The village isn't. They know what they saw: both hands.
[d97] SAY: ◈ OUTSHONE — Logan Zielinski did what you would not: acted, boldly, while there was still time. The village thrives. The god is someone else. The record notes, kindly and accurately, that you watched. It stings exactly as much as it should.
[d97] SAY: The terms held — the table had priced the village, ascension and all, and the village paid in full.
